Direct `corr_shifts(h)`:
```
def corr_shifts(h):
    n=len(h)
    oneh = 1-h
    res = np.empty(2*n-1)
    for s in range(-(n-1), n):
        if s>=0:
            res[n-1+s] = np.dot(h[:n-s], oneh[s:])
        else:
            res[n-1+s] = np.dot(h[-s:], oneh[:n+s])
    return res
```

```
    n=len(h)
    L=2*n-1
    off=n-1
    oneh=1-h
    zero_idx=np.where(h<0.5)[0]

        row=np.zeros(L)
        # left: s where i+s in [0,n-1] => s in [-i, n-1-i]
        lo=-i; hi=n-1-i
        if lo <= hi:
            vals = oneh[i+lo:i+hi+1] # should work lo can negative? numpy slicing with negative tricky.
```

```
        s = np.arange(lo, hi+1)
        idx = i+s
        row[off+s] = oneh[idx]

        lo2=i-(n-1); hi2=i
```

```
def find_best_swap(h, corr, tol=0):
    n=len(h); off=n-1; L=2*n-1; cur=corr.max()
    zeros = np.flatnonzero(h==0)
    ones = np.flatnonzero(h==1)
    if len(zeros)==0 or len(ones)==0: return None
    oneh = 1-h
    # Build V for ones: maybe we can also not build all U, build V matrix; for each zero compute U row.
    V = np.empty((len(ones), L), dtype=np.int32)
    for bi,j in enumerate(ones): ... V[bi]=row
    best=new_c=cur
    best_pair=None
    for i in zeros:
       U = zero_row(i)
       add = U + corr  # length L
       # base max over ones rows: V + add
       # We can compute M = (V + add[None,:]).max(axis=1)
       # point arrays
       pos_lags = off + ones - i
       neg_lags = off + i - ones
       vals = np.maximum(V.max axis... )? base = (V + add).max(axis=1)
       point0 = V[:,off] + add[off] - 2
       pointp = V[:,pos_lags] + add[pos_lags] + 1
       pointen = V[:,neg_lags] + add[neg_lags] +1
       exact = np.maximum.reduce([base, point0, pointp, pointen])
       # to improve, exact < cur - tol
       jrel = int(np.argmax(exact - cur)) or `idx = np.where(exact < cur - tol)` pick min exact
       if exact.min() < cur - tol:
          ist = int(np.argmin(exact))
          if ist < best_new: best_new=exact[ist]; best_pair=(int(i), int(ones[ist]))
    if best_pair is None: return None
    return best_pair, best_new
```

Let's design a `binary_local_steepest(h, corr, max_time, max_moves)` top-level:
```
def binary_local_steepest(h, time_limit, min_improve=1e-12):
    h = np.asarray(h).copy()
    n=len(h)
    target=n//2
    # ensure integral by adjusting binary count
    h = make_binary(h,n)
    corr = corr_shifts(h)
    cur = corr.max()
    t0=time.time()
    moves=0
    rng = np.random.RandomState(12345)
    while time.time() < time_limit and moves < 100000:
        best = find_best_swap(h, corr)
        if best is None:
            # maybe do random perturb if not at time?
            break
        i,j,newc = best
        if newc >= cur - 1e-9: break
        # apply
        delta = compute_swap_delta(h,i,j)
        corr += delta
        h[i],h[j]=1,0
        cur = newc  # should equal corr.max()
        moves+=1
    # maybe random perturbation and repeat?
```
But `find_best_swap` can be expensive; if no improving after some moves, we need escape. We can add perturbations with penalty: If stuck, do random k swaps (e.g., 5-10) accepting worse, then continue. But random may go far; restart from parent is safer. We'll use multiple starts.

`make_binary(h,n)`:
```
def make_binary(h,n):
    target=n//2
    vals=np.asarray(h,float)
    # if almost binary
    rb=np.where(vals>0.5,1.0,0.0)
    s=rb.sum()
    if abs(s-target) < 1e-12: return rb
    # sort by values descending, top target
    order=np.argsort(-vals, kind='stable')
    out=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    out[order[:target]]=1.0
    return out
```
For parent fractional, this chooses top values. Could damage symmetry; but local search can fix. We could also use threshold 0.5 if sum close. Use exact target.

Pseudo:
```
def binary_local(h, time_limit, random_perturb=0, seed=0):
    h = np.asarray(h,float).copy()
    n=len(h)
    hb = make_binary(h,n)
    corr = corr_shifts(hb)
    cur = corr.max()
    best_h=hb.copy(); best_cur=cur
    t0=time.time(); rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    move_count=0
    # We'll loop: while time < limit:
    while time.time() + 1 < time_limit:
        # find best
        res = find_best_swap(hb, corr)
        if res is None:
            # try random perturbation if allowed
            if random_perturb and move_count>0? 
               do k random swaps; recompute; maybe continue
            else: break
        i,j,newcur=res
        # apply and recompute
        hb[i],hb[j]=1.0,0.0
        corr=corr_shifts(hb); cur=corr.max()
        if cur < best_cur - 1e-9:
            best_cur=cur; best_h=hb.copy()
        move_count+=1
    # maybe after local, try a few 2-swap perturb and local? (handled by outer)
    c5 = best_cur * (2.0/n)
    return best_h, c5, n
```

`apply_swap` computing delta exact from h before flip:
```
def swap_delta(h,i,j):
    n=len(h); off=n-1; L=2n-1
    oneh=1-h
    h_rev=h[::-1]
    delta=np.zeros(L, dtype=np.int64)
    # U_i
    delta[off-i:off-i+n] += oneh
    delta[off+i-n+1:off+i+1] -= h_rev
    # V_j
    delta[off-j:off-j+n] -= oneh
    delta[off+j-n+1:off+j+1] += h_rev
    delta[off] -=2
    delta[off+j-i] +=1
    delta[off+i-j] +=1
    return delta
```
Wait U_i left uses oneh before flip. If i zero, oneh[i]=1; okay. V_j right uses h_rev before flip. Good.
But note if i to 1, j to 0, the `h_rev` and `oneh` are old. Slices include changed positions old. Correct. After adding delta, corr kept as float: `corr = corr + delta.astype(float)`.
Then recompute `corr_shifts` every e.g. 10 moves to correct. But if we don't recompute, find_best_swap profiles use old h; okay current h. corr should be exact if deltas exact. - Initialize `corr = corr_shifts(h).astype(np.int64)` (round). Because h binary. Then corr exact int. `find_best_swap` uses int arrays; output int. `corr += delta` int. No drift. Periodic recompute maybe to ensure. Use `corr_shifts_int` returning int.
Let's implement `corr_shifts` with dtype float; for binary we can `np.rint`. For local, use int64. For final c5, compute float.
```
def corr_shifts_int(h):
   res=np.empty(L,dtype=np.int64)
   for s ...: res[off+s]=int(round(np.dot(h[:len],1-h[len:])))
   return res
```
`h` binary float; dot of 0/1 float; round. If parent fractional, make_binary first.

def steepest_repeated(h, time_limit, max_moves=1000000):

    corr = corr_shifts_int(hb)
    cur = int(corr.max())

        hb_int? hb.astype? Need keep int8.

        cur=res_cur

           cur = int(corr.max())

```
`find_best_swap` implementation with buffers:
```
def find_best_swap(h, corr):
    n=len(h); off=n-1; L=2*n-1
    zeros = np.flatnonzero(h==0); ones=np.flatnonzero(h==1)
    z=zeros.astype(np.int64); o=ones.astype(np.int64)
    m=ones.size
    if m==0: return None
    oneh = 1 - h
    hrev = h[::-1]
    V = np.empty((m,L), dtype=np.int32)
    for bi,j in enumerate(o):
        j=int(j); row=V[bi]; row[:] = 0
        row[off-j:off-j+n] -= oneh
        row[off+j-n+1:off+j+1] += hrev
    cur = int(corr.max())
    U = np.empty(L,dtype=np.int32); add=np.empty(L,dtype=np.int32)
    basenc=np.empty(m,dtype=np.int32)
    best_val = cur+1  # no improvement
    best_i=-1; best_j=-1; best_index=0
    for ai,i in enumerate(z):
       i=int(i)
       U[:] = 0
       U[off-i:off-i+n] += oneh
       U[off+i-n+1:off+i+1] -= hrev
       np.add(corr, U, out=add)
       pos_lags = off + (o - i) # int64 array length m
       neg_lags = off + (i - o)
       np.add(V, add, out=D?) Need D buffer.

```
`swap_delta` implementation with int8 h:
```
def swap_delta(h,i,j):
    n=len(h); off=n-1; L=2*n-1
    delta = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.int32)
    oneh = 1 - h
    hrev = h[::-1]
    delta[off-i:off-i+n] += oneh
    delta[off+i-n+1:off+i+1] -= hrev
    delta[off-j:off-j+n] -= oneh
    delta[off+j-n+1:off+j+1] += hrev
    delta[off] -= 2
    delta[off+j-i] += 1
    delta[off+i-j] += 1
    return delta
```
Check if slices overlap and order okay; integer in-place. Good.
For steepest update, we can call `delta=swap_delta(hb,i,j)`, then `corr += delta`, `hb[i]=1; hb[j]=0`. But note `swap_delta` uses old h, and `hb` should still old. Good.

For SA, we can reuse `swap_delta` but preallocate `delta_buf` to avoid allocation. However `swap_delta` allocates new each trial. We can write `add_swap_delta_to_buffer(h, i, j, delta_buf)` top-level:
```
def prepare_swap_delta(h,i,j, delta):
    delta[:] = 0; n=... ; off=...
    oneh=1-h; hrev=h[::-1]


    n=len(h)

    corr = corr_shifts_int(hb)

    best_h = hb.copy(); best_cur=cur

    tmp = np.empty(2*n-1, dtype=np.int32)

    # temperature schedule: start maybe 3.0, end 0.0 over time. We can use elapsed fraction.

        # anneal over time

```
`gradient_max_counts(h,alpha)`:
Use weights w (sum1) length L. Compute grad as described. Let's implement direct loops over lags to avoid convolution complexity:
```
def grad_counts(h,w):
   n=len(h); grad=np.zeros(n)
   for s in range(-(n-1), n):
      ws=w[n-1+s]
      if abs(ws)<1e-10: continue
      if s>=0:
          L=n-s
          # left contrib: grad[:L] += ws*(1-h[s:])
          grad[:L] += ws*(1.0 - h[s:])
          # right contrib: grad[s:] -= ws*h[:L]
          grad[s:] -= ws*h[:L]
      else:
          t=-s
          L=n-t
          # left endpoints j=t..n-1 with h[j-t]
          grad[t:] += ws*(1.0 - h[:L])
          # right endpoints j=0..n-t-1 with h[j+t]
          grad[:L] -= ws*h[t:]
   return grad
```
Let's test for s=0: L=n; grad += w(1-h) - w*h = w(1-2h). Good.
For s>0: Example n=2, h=[a,b]. T_s: s=1: a(1-b). Derivative wrt a=1-b, wrt b=-a. Loop: grad[:1] += w(1-b) yes; grad[1:] += -w*a yes. Good.
For s=-1: T_{-1}=b(1-a)? Our convention c_s=sum h_i(1-h_{i-1}) for i>=1: b(1-a). Deriv a=-b, b=1-a. Loop s=-1,t=1,L=1: grad[1:] += w(1-a); grad[:1] -= w*b. Good.
Great.

`project_box_sum` bisection:
```
def project_box_sum(x,target):
    x=np.clip(x,0,1).astype(float)
    s=x.sum()
    if abs(s-target)<1e-12: return x
    lo=np.min(x)-1.0; hi=np.max(x)+1.0
    # increasing lambda decreases sum? h=clip(x-lambda,0,1). As lambda increases, sum decreases.
    for _ in range(80):
       mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
       y=np.clip(x-mid,0,1)
       sy=y.sum()
       if sy > target:
          lo=mid
       else:
          hi=mid
    return np.clip(x-mid,0,1)
```
Need after loop ensure target? If all x 0/1 and target integer, projection may choose threshold; bisection fine. For exact binary, if target integer and x values near 0/1, could sum off due lambda; but binary local handles. We can if sum diff small adjust random.
```

def to_binary_array(h):
    n=len(h); target=n//2
    arr=np.asarray(h,float)
    # threshold
    rb = (arr > 0.5).astype(np.int8)
    if rb.sum()==target:
        return rb
    order=np.argsort(-arr, kind='stable')
    out=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.int8)
    out[order[:target]]=1
    return out

def corr_shifts_int(h):
    n=len(h); off=n-1; L=2*n-1
    res=np.empty(L,dtype=np.int32)
    # h int8 0/1, oneh = 1-h
    oneh = 1 - h

        if s>=0:
           ln=n-s
           val=np.dot(h[:ln], oneh[s:s+ln])
        else:
           t=-s; ln=n-t
           val=np.dot(h[t:t+ln? Wait for s<0: c_s=sum_{i=0}^{n-1} h[i]*(1-h[i+s]) where i+s>=0 -> i>=t. h[i], oneh[i+t? no i+s = i-t]. For s=-t, valid i=t..n-1: h[i] oneh[i-t]. So arrays h[t:n] length n-t and oneh[0:n-t].
           val=np.dot(h[t:], oneh[:ln])
        res[off+s] = int(np.rint(val))
    return res

def make_int_binary(x):
    n=len(x)
    target = n // 2
    arr=np.asarray(x,float)
    rb=(arr>=0.5).astype(np.int8)
    if int(rb.sum())==target: return rb
    # To preserve as much, sort desc; if exactly threshold and sum too high/low, use random tie? Simple order.
    order=np.argsort(-arr, kind='stable')
    out=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.int8)
    out[order[:target]]=1
    return out

def steepest_repeated(h, time_limit, max_moves=None, recompute_every=32):

```
`while time.time()+1 < time_limit:`
   if moves>max_moves break.
   res=find_best_swap(hb,corr)
   if res is None: break
   i,j,val=res
```

`make_initial_fallback(n)`:
```
def make_initial_fallback(n):
    n=int(n)
    h=np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*2.0/n
    h[(x<0.25) | ((x>=0.75)&(x<1.25)) | (x>=1.75)] = 1.0
    target=n//2
    s=int(h.sum())
    if s > target:
       idx=np.argsort(-h) # zeros at end? better set some 0
       h[idx[max(0,s-target):]]=0? Actually idx descending values => top ones. set first s-target to 0.
       cnt=s-target
       for ii in range(cnt): h[idx[ii]]=0
    elif s < target:
       idx=np.argsort(h) # zeros first
       for ii in range(target-s): h[idx[ii]]=1
    return h
```
But if n odd target floor; fallback continuous? For even okay.

`resample_h(h,n)`:
Use midpoints. If n==old, return copy. If new even? okay.
```
def resample_h(h, n):
    old_n=len(h)
    if old_n == n: return np.asarray(h,float).copy()
    x_old=(np.arange(old_n)+0.5)*2.0/old_n
    x_new=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*2.0/n
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, np.asarray(h,float))
```
For binary repeated, separate `replicate_h(h, factor)` returns np.repeat. For n=2*old exactly.

`to_binary_array` as above.
`project_continuous` as above. With exact sum target. Need if target not integer and x proj with bisection may hit boundary? n odd target not integer; bisection fine.

`c5_of(h)`:
```
def c5_of(h):
    h=np.asarray(h,float)
    n=len(h)
    if n==0: return 1.0
    # ensure clip? not modify
    c=np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, mode='full')* (2.0/n)
    return float(np.max(c))
```
If h is int8, 1.0-h float.

`ensure_feasible(h)`:
```
def ensure_feasible(h):
    h=np.asarray(h,float)
    n=len(h)
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    target=float(n)/2.0
    if abs(h.sum()-target) > 1e-7:
       h=project_continuous(h)
    return h
```
But project_continuous uses target n/2; for even binary sum exact. For n odd, target .5. okay.

`swap_delta` top-level as above. It returns int32. Make sure slices valid:
- left zero: `off-i` can be negative? off=n-1, i up to n-1 => off-i >=0. End off-i+n <= off+n =2n-1? If i=0, end=2n-1, okay last exclusive. So valid.
- right zero: `off+i-n+1` = i, because off=n-1 => n-1+i-n+1=i. End off+i+1 = n-1+i+1 = n+i. For i=n-1, end=2n-1. Length n. Valid. Actually right segment start=i, end=n+i. Great simplify: right segment always `[i : n+i]` includes reversed h. left segment `[off-i : off-i+n] = [n-1-i : n-1-i+n]`. Good.
For one V: left `[off-j:off-j+n] -= oneh`; right `[j:n+j] += hrev`.
Delta: `delta[off-i : off-i+n] += oneh`, `delta[i : n+i] -= hrev`; `delta[off-j : off-j+n] -= oneh`, `delta[j : n+j] += hrev`; corrections `off`, `off+j-i`, `off+i-j` valid. Good. This simplifies.

`prepare_delta` for SA can use same slices with delta buffer.

`find_best_swap` with buffers:
Need allocate `V` each call; if many moves, repeated allocation. Could use `np.empty` each time. Fine.
Let's write:
```
def find_best_swap(h, corr):
    n = len(h)
    off = n - 1
    L = 2*n - 1
    zeros = np.flatnonzero(h == 0)
    ones = np.flatnonzero(h == 1)
    mzero=...; mone=...
    if mzero == 0 or mone == 0: return None
    oneh = 1 - h
    hrev = h[::-1]
    V = np.empty((mone, L), dtype=np.int32)
    for bi in range(mone):
        j=int(ones[bi]); row=V[bi]; row[:]=0
        row[off-j : off-j+n] -= oneh
        row[j : n+j] += hrev
    cur = int(corr.max())
    D = np.empty((mone, L), dtype=np.int32)

    best_val = cur
    best_i = -1; best_j = -1
    # strict improvement requires best_val < cur; initialize best_val = cur
    for ai in range(mzero):
        i=int(zeros[ai])
        U[:]=0
        U[off-i : off-i+n] += oneh
        U[i : n+i] -= hrev
        np.add(corr, U, out=add)
        pos_lags = off + (ones - i)  # ones array int8? ones int64 from flatnonzero; okay
        neg_lags = off + (i - ones)
        np.add(V, add, out=D)
        # point0 buffer
        pt[:] = V[:, off]
        pt += add[off] - 2
        D[:, off] = SENT
        np.max(D, axis=1, out=bas)
        # pos
        np.add(V[:, pos_lags], add[pos_lags], out=pt)
        pt += 1
        np.maximum(bas, pt, out=bas)
        # neg
        np.add(V[:, neg_lags], add[neg_lags], out=pt)
        pt += 1
        np.maximum(bas, pt, out=bas)
        # point0
        np.maximum(bas, pt_zero, out=bas)? We used pt overwritten. Need save point0 separate.
```

```
def steepest_repeated(h, deadline, max_moves=1000000):
    n=len(h)

    while time.time() + 0.1 < deadline:
       res=find_best_swap(hb, corr)
       if res is None: break
       i,j,val=res

       hb[i]=1; hb[j]=0
       corr += delta
```

`simulate_binary`:
```
def simulate_binary(h, deadline, seed, tc0=0.005):
    n=len(h)
    hb=to_binary_array(h)
    dx=2.0/n
    corr=corr_shifts_int(hb)
    cur=int(corr.max()); cur_c=cur*dx
    best_h=hb.copy(); best_c=cur_c
    zeros=np.flatnonzero(hb==0).astype(np.int64).copy()
    ones=np.flatnonzero(hb==1).astype(np.int64).copy()
    oneh=1-hb  # int8
    # hrev view? We'll use hb[::-1] each trial? That creates view, okay. But in slices, `hb[::-1]` maybe computed each time? We'll assign `hrev = hb[::-1]`; view updates.
    hrev = hb[::-1]
    delta = np.empty(2*n-1, dtype=np.int32)
    tmp = np.empty(2*n-1, dtype=np.int32)
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    t0=time.time(); total=max(deadline-t0,1e-9)
    while time.time() < deadline - 0.02:
       frac=(time.time()-t0)/total
       if frac>=1: break
       T=tc0 * (1.0 - frac)**2
       a=int(rng.randint(zeros.size)); i=int(zeros[a])
       b=int(rng.randint(ones.size)); j=int(ones[b])
       delta[:] = 0
       # slices
       delta[off-i:off-i+n] += oneh
       delta[i:n+i] -= hrev
       delta[off-j:off-j+n] -= oneh
       delta[j:n+j] += hrev
       delta[off] -= 2
       delta[off+j-i] += 1
       delta[off+i-j] += 1
       np.add(corr, delta, out=tmp)
       newmax = int(tmp.max())
       new_c = newmax*dx
       accept=False
       if new_c < cur_c:
           accept=True
       elif new_c < cur_c + T:
           accept=True
       if accept:
           # update h/oneh/arrays
           hb[i]=1; hb[j]=0
           oneh[i]=0; oneh[j]=1
           zeros[a]=j; ones[b]=i
           corr += delta
           cur=newmax; cur_c=new_c
           if cur_c < best_c:
               best_c=cur_c; best_h=hb.copy()
       # optional: if best_c very low, maybe reduce T? schedule as is
    return best_h.astype(float), best_c
```
Potential bug: `hrev = hb[::-1]` is a view. When we update `hb[i]=1`, the view reflects update at position `n-1-i`. Good. For `delta[i:n+i] -= hrev`, uses current oneh/hrev old before accept. Good.
After accept, `delta` no longer needed; next trial zero. `corr += delta` int32; if corr values can exceed int32? no.
If `zeros.size` or `ones.size` zero? For even n with sum n/2, both nonzero unless n=0. Good.
If h not even sum, to_binary_array ensures target floor for even; if odd n? We should not call on odd. In run ensure even.
Temperature: If no movement for long due T cooling too fast, best stays. Multiple SA bursts in wrap with new t0 restart temperature. Good.

Potential issue: In SA, if accept a move with new_c > cur_c, cur_c increases; T decays based on time, not current. It may wander far. But schedule ends downhill. Track best.

Now, `binary_search_wrap`:
```
def binary_search_wrap(h_init, deadline, seed, steepest_burst=30, sa_burst=45):
    n=len(h_init)
    if n%2 !=0:
        n += 1
        h_init = resample_h(h_init, n)
    h0 = to_binary_array(h_init).astype(float)
    best_h = h0.copy()
    best_c = c5_of(best_h)
    current_h = h0.copy()
    attempt=0
    can_steepest = n <= 600
    # initial time check
    while time.time() < deadline - 3:
        attempt += 1
        # steepest burst from current_h (maybe best)
        if can_steepest and time.time() < deadline - 10:
            end = min(deadline - 2, time.time() + steepest_burst)
            h_out, cnt = steepest_repeated(current_h, end)
            cval = cnt * (2.0/n)
            if cval < best_c - 1e-12:
                best_c = cval; best_h = h_out.copy(); current_h = h_out.copy()
        # SA from current best or current
        if time.time() < deadline - 5:
            # use current_h if it's already local? use best_h
            start_h = best_h.copy()
            end = min(deadline - 1, time.time() + sa_burst)
            h_out, csa = simulate_binary(start_h, end, seed + attempt*101)
            if csa < best_c - 1e-12:
                best_c=csa; best_h=h_out.copy(); current_h=h_out.copy()
            # optionally perturb if time left
            if time.time() < deadline - max(10, sa_burst/2):
                k = 3 + (attempt % 4)
                start_h = random_k_swaps(best_h, k, seed + 1000 + attempt)
                end = min(deadline -1, time.time() + sa_burst*0.7)
                h_out, csa = simulate_binary(start_h, end, seed + 2000 + attempt)
                if csa < best_c - 1e-12:
                    best_c=csa; best_h=h_out.copy(); current_h=h_out.copy()
                # maybe steepest after SA
                if can_steepest and time.time() < deadline - 8:
                    end = min(deadline -2, time.time() + steepest_burst*0.6)
                    h_out, cnt = steepest_repeated(current_h, end)
                    cval=cnt*2.0/n
                    if cval < best_c - 1e-12:
                        best_c=cval; best_h=h_out.copy(); current_h=h_out.copy()
    return best_h, best_c
```
This uses `steepest_repeated` with `current_h` maybe after SA. Good.
But note `steepest_repeated` returns count cmax, not C5; ensure `cnt` int count; multiply 2/n. If h was current_h maybe not best, but if it doesn't improve best, no update. Good.
Potential infinite loop? Time decreases, so finite. If bursts very short, still while time.

If n=1000, no steepest; only SA. `simulate_binary` can handle 1000: delta length 1999, per trial operations O(n) with slices and `np.add(corr,delta)` O(n), max O(n); maybe 1000*2000=2000 operations? Actually np.add and max over 1999 int32 ~2000, plus slices 4*1000; maybe 6000 C ops plus Python overhead. 30s maybe 100k trials? Good.

`random_k_swaps(h,k,seed)`: If h float, convert int. Need ensure k not too large relative n. Use total pairs target*(n-target). If k > max steps/10? Fine.
```
def random_k_swaps(h, k, seed):
    hb=to_binary_array(h).astype(np.int8)
    zeros=np.flatnonzero(hb==0).astype(np.int64).copy()
    ones=np.flatnonzero(hb==1).astype(np.int64).copy()
    if zeros.size==0 or ones.size==0: return hb.astype(float)
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    for _ in range(k):
       if zeros.size ==0 or ones.size==0: break
       a=int(rng.randint(zeros.size)); b=int(rng.randint(ones.size))
       i=int(zeros[a]); j=int(ones[b])
       hb[i]=1; hb[j]=0
       zeros[a]=j; ones[b]=i
    return hb.astype(float)
```

`prepare_resolution(h_new, n_target)`:
```
def prepare_resolution(h, n_target):
    n_old=len(h)
    if n_target == n_old: return np.asarray(h,float).copy()
    # if binary all close
    arr=np.asarray(h,float)
    if np.all(arr >= -1e-8) and np.all(arr <= 1+1e-8):
        b=(arr>0.5).astype(float)
        if np.allclose(arr,b,atol=1e-8):
            if n_target % n_old == 0:
                factor = n_target // n_old
                if factor>=1: return np.repeat(b, factor)
    return resample_h(arr,n_target)
```
Use for phase starts.

We can implement exact objective and gradient for soft max with alpha. This can be much faster than finite diff. Let's design SLSQP with analytic gradient.

SLSQP minimizes objective `f(x) = max_k T_k` approximated by smoothed `soft = M + (1/α) log Σ exp(α(T_k - M))`, where T_k counts (no dx). Constraints: sum x = n/2; bounds 0≤x≤1. We can define objective and gradient (soft) for SLSQP. SLSQP will use them. Alpha large approximates max. We can run with increasing alpha and from parent. This is similar to previous SLSQP but with analytic gradient and no finite diff, maybe better.

Need compute `T` for all lags and gradient wrt x efficiently for SLSQP. n=250, L=499. Direct loop over lags for T O(n^2); gradient O(n^2) or using prefix/convolution. SLSQP iterations maybe hundreds; O(n^2) 62k per iter, fine. We can implement efficient enough.

Let's implement `smooth_c_and_grad(h, alpha, return_weights=False)`:
- Compute c counts (float) via `c = corr_shifts_float(h)`.
- M=np.max(c). temp=1/alpha. z=(c-M)/temp. To avoid underflow: `z = np.maximum(z, -700)`. `w = np.exp(z); w /= w.sum()`. Values: if alpha huge, w active only. Sum1.
- Objective soft = M + np.log(w.sum())*temp? Since w unnormalized exp(z); logsumexp = M/temp + log(sum exp z)? Let's compute: z=(c-M)/temp; sumexp = Σ exp(z). logsumexp(c/temp) = M/temp + log(sumexp). Soft objective for minimizing max: `soft = temp * (M/temp + log sumexp) = M + temp*log(sumexp)`. Actually if temp=1/α, soft = max + temp*log(exp((c-M)/temp) sum). Yes. Multiply by dx for C? Since dx constant, minimize counts. We can return soft_count. For SLSQP, any positive scale; use counts.
- Gradient wrt h: `g = Σ_s w_s ∂T_s/∂h`. Derive loop as above with weights w. Since w sum1. Use np zeros n float.
Loop over s in [-n+1,n-1]: if w nonzero; for s>=0 L=n-s: grad[:L] += ws*(1-h[s:]); grad[s:] -= ws*h[:L]. For s<0 t=-s L=n-t: grad[t:] += ws*(1-h[:L]); grad[:L] -= ws*h[t:]. This is O(n^2). Good.
Need include c_s normalization? We use counts; objective soft in counts. dx constant not needed. If using C5, multiply by dx; same gradient.

SLSQP call:
`minimize(fun, x0, jac, method='SLSQP', bounds, constraints=[{'type':'eq','fun':sum-h?}], options={'maxiter':..., 'ftol':...})`
Need no closures. Define top-level functions:
```
def slsqp_obj(x, alpha):
   val, _ = soft_c_grad(x, alpha)
   return val
def slsqp_grad(x, alpha):
   _,g = soft_c_grad(x, alpha)
   return g
```
But SLSQP requires functions with one arg; alpha would need closure or global. Can't use closures. We can use global variable? We can set module-level global `_ALPHA`? But in harness top-level? We can define a function that reads a global set in run: `global SLSQP_ALPHA; SLSQP_ALPHA=alpha`. But multiple runs? `run` may be called once. Global acceptable. Or pass alpha via `kwargs` using `functools.partial` not allowed? Could use a class? Simpler: Use global variable `_current_alpha`. Top-level `def smooth_objective_theta(x): return smooth_c(x, _current_alpha)[0]`. Set global in run. Need declare global in setter? We can define helper:
```
def set_alpha(a):
    global _ALPHA
    _ALPHA = float(a)
```

```
def smooth_slsqp(h0, alpha, maxiter, deadline):
    global _ALPHA, _TARGET
    h = np.asarray(h0,float)

    target=n/2.0

    set_alpha(alpha); set_target(target)
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)] for i in range(n)

    if res.x feasible? project
    return project_continuous(res.x)
```

```
def soft_c_and_grad(h, alpha):
    c=corr_shifts_float(h)
    M=c.max()
    temp=1.0/alpha
    z=(c-M)/temp
    # stable: if temp <=0? alpha huge; use active
    if temp < 1e-12:
       w=np.zeros(2*n-1); w[np.isclose(c,M)] = 1/...
    else:
       z=np.clip(z, -700, 700) # max 0
       expz=np.exp(z)
       s=expz.sum()
       w=expz/s
    soft = M + temp*np.log(s) if not under...
    grad=weighted_gradient(h,w)
    return soft, grad
```

`corr_shifts_float`:
```
def corr_shifts_float(h):
    n=len(h); off=n-1; L=2*n-1; res=np.empty(L,float)
    oneh=1.0-h
    for s in range(-(n-1), n):
       if s>=0: ln=n-s; res[off+s]=np.dot(h[:ln], oneh[s:s+ln])
       else: t=-s; ln=n-t; res[off+s]=np.dot(h[t:], oneh[:ln])
    return res
```
For s negative, h[t:t+ln] length ln; oneh[:ln] length ln. Good.

`weighted_gradient` as loops. Use `w` float. Could use `np.dot` with weights? Loop 2n. For alpha large and many weights zero, skip.
```
def weighted_gradient(h,w):
    n=len(h); grad=np.zeros(n,float)
    for s in range(-(n-1), n):
       ws=float(w[n-1+s])
       if ws == 0.0: continue
       if s >=0:
          ln=n-s
          grad[:ln] += ws * (1.0 - h[s:s+ln])
          if ln>0: grad[s:] -= ws * h[:ln]
       else:
          t=-s; ln=n-t
          grad[t:] += ws * (1.0 - h[:ln])
          grad[:ln] -= ws * h[t:]
    return grad
```
For s=0: first grad[:n] += w(1-h); second grad[0:] -= w*h -> w(1-2h). Good.

`soft_c_and_grad` if alpha <=0: alpha=1. For very large alpha, temp small. To avoid `np.exp` of z with -inf? clamp.
```
z=(c-M)/temp
z=np.clip(z, -745, 0.0) # max 0
e=np.exp(z)
s=e.sum()
if s <=0: # only active
   active=np.isclose(c,M, rtol=0, atol=1e-12); w=0; w[active]=1/active.sum()
   soft=M
else:
   w=e/s; soft=M+temp*np.log(s)
```
Since z max 0, logsumexp = log(s). Good.

But `smooth_slsqp` should use global alpha; we can set inside. To avoid alpha global conflict, define `slsqp_run_once(x0, alpha, maxiter)`. It sets global.
```
def slsqp_run_once(x0, alpha, maxiter):
    global _ALPHA,_TARGET
    _ALPHA=alpha; _TARGET=len(x0)/2.0
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*len(x0)
    cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':slsq_constraint}]
    try:
       res=minimize(slsq_objective, x0, jac=slsq_gradient, method='SLSQP', bounds=bounds, constraints=cons, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-14})
       return project_continuous(res.x)
    except Exception:
       return project_continuous(x0)
```

In SLSQP, if alpha is global but objective uses it, okay.

```
def slsqp_phase(h_init, deadline, seed):
    n=...; h=project_continuous(h_init); best...
    rng=RandomState(seed)
    alphas_cycle = [10,30,100,300,1000,3000]

        # choose small perturbation amplitude decays with iterations
        amp = 0.05 * max(0.2, 0.5**restart_count?)
        x0 = project_continuous(best_h + amp*rng.randn(n)) if restart_count>0 else best_h.copy()

        h1=slsqp_run_once(x0,alpha,maxit)
        c1=c5_of(h1)
        if c1 < best_c: update
        restart_count +=1; idx +=1
    return best
```

Let's implement `slsqp_phase`:
```
def slsqp_phase(h_init, deadline, seed):
    n=len(h_init)
    if n > 400: return h_init, c5_of(h_init)
    h=project_continuous(h_init)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c5_of(h)
    # first chain
    chain=[(20,100),(50,150),(100,200),(200,200),(500,150),(1000,120)]
    for alpha,maxit in chain:
        if time.time() > deadline - 5: break
        h1=slsqp_run_once(h, alpha, maxit)
        c1=c5_of(h1)
        if c1 < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_c=c1; best_h=h1.copy(); h=h1.copy()
        else:
            # keep h from result? maybe result may be worse but closer? use best
            h=best_h.copy()
    # restarts
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    count=0
    while time.time() < deadline - 5:
       count+=1
       amp=0.01 + 0.03*rng.rand()
       if count%5==0: amp=0.08
       x0=project_continuous(best_h + amp*rng.randn(n))
       alpha=[200,1000,50,1000,50][count%5]
       maxit=150
       h1=slsqp_run_once(x0,alpha,maxit)
       c1=c5_of(h1)
       if c1 < best_c - 1e-14:
          best_c=c1; best_h=h1.copy()
    return best_h,best_c
```
Note `h=best_h.copy()` in chain ensures next from best. But if result worse, maybe still helpful? Use best.

Now, let's think about constraints: The task says sum(h)*dx=1 exactly (sum == n/2 exactly). For continuous SLSQP, our projection bisection yields sum close but not exact. The evaluator may not require exact, but constraints state exactly. To be safe, after final project, adjust sum to exact integer/half? For n even, target integer. If h fractional, we can make sum exact by distributing tiny epsilon among non-boundary elements. After `project_continuous`, sum error maybe 1e-9. We can correct:
```
def exact_sum_fix(h):
    n=len(h); target=n/2.0
    s=h.sum(); diff=target-s
    if abs(diff) < 1e-12: return h
    # add diff to elements away from bounds
    mask=(h>1e-8)&(h<1-1e-8)
    if mask.sum()>0:
       h[mask] += diff/mask.sum(); h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    else:
       # distribute to all with small movements
       h = h + diff/n; clip
    # iterative few times
    for _ in range(20):
       diff=target-h.sum()
       if abs(diff)<1e-15: break
       mask=(h>1e-12)&(h<1-1e-12)
       if mask.sum()>0:
          h[mask]+=diff/mask.sum()
       else:
          break
       h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    return h
```
But adding to subset changes sum; after clip maybe. We can implement `fix_sum_exact(h)`.
In final `update_best` or before return. For binary, exact. For continuous, use fix.
However adding tiny amounts may change C by <1e-9. Good.

In `project_continuous`, bisection 100 iterations should get sum error ~1e-12. Good.

Now, could the evaluator recompute C5 using `np.correlate(h, 1-h, mode='full') * dx` with h maybe having sum slightly off? We ensure.

Let's include `fix_sum_exact` and call in `ensure_feasible`.
```
def ensure_feasible(h):
   h=np.asarray(h,float)
   n=len(h); target=n/2.0
   h=np.clip(h,0,1)
   if abs(h.sum()-target)>1e-10:
       h=project_continuous(h)
   h=fix_sum_exact(h)
   return h
```

`fix_sum_exact` top-level:
```
def fix_sum_exact(h):
    h=np.array(h,float,copy=True)
    target=float(len(h))/2.0
    for _ in range(50):
       diff=target-h.sum()
       if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
       mask=(h>1e-12)&(h<1.0-1e-12)
       cnt=int(mask.sum())
       if cnt>0:
          h[mask]+=diff/cnt
       else:
          # if all at bounds, cannot adjust exactly; distribute to boundary? leave.
          break
       np.clip(h,0.0,1.0,out=h)
    return h
```
If n odd target .5, mask can adjust. If all h 0/1 and sum off by 0.5 impossible, leave. But we avoid odd binary.

Let's detail `prepare_resolution`:
```
def prepare_resolution(h, n_target):
    h=np.asarray(h,float)
    n_old=len(h)
    n_target=int(n_target)
    if n_target <=0: return h.copy()
    if n_old == n_target: return h.copy()
    # if binary and divisible
    if n_target % n_old == 0:
        bmask = (h > 0.5).astype(float)
        if np.allclose(h, bmask, atol=1e-8, rtol=0.0):
            return np.repeat(bmask, n_target // n_old)
    return resample_h(h,n_target)
```
If h has values exactly 0/1 but with sum not exact, binary mask. Good.

`update_best`: If candidate is binary int8/float and n even, to_binary? No, don't alter if continuous. But if n even and candidate nearly binary but sum off? If c5 lower but infeasible sum, fix. We'll call `ensure_feasible`? It may project continuous and change C. For binary, if sum target, unchanged. For continuous, if sum not target, project. Then `fix_sum_exact`. But `ensure_feasible` calls `project_continuous` if sum error >1e-9, which may move slightly. Then c5 of changed. We want candidate evaluated if it was feasible; if not, project. Good.
```
def update_best(hcand, best_h, best_c, best_n):
    cand=np.asarray(hcand,float)
    n=len(cand)
    cand=ensure_feasible(cand)
    c=c5_of(cand)
    if c < best_c - 1e-14:
       return cand, c, n
    return best_h,best_c,best_n
```
For binary candidate, ensure_feasible may `project_continuous` if sum not exactly; if binary n even sum exact, okay. If sum diff tiny, project? We check >1e-7 in ensure? We'll set >1e-7. Good.

`ensure_feasible`:
```
def ensure_feasible(h):
    h=np.asarray(h,float)
    n=len(h)
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    target=n/2.0
    if abs(h.sum()-target) > 1e-7:
       h=project_continuous(h)
    h=fix_sum_exact(h)
    return h
```
For binary n=250 sum exact, fix no change. SLSQP fractional sum maybe exact within 1e-7.

Now, `project_continuous` target n/2. If n odd and input continuous, bisection can find. If n even and h all 0/1 but sum off, bisection with threshold may output fractional; okay.
Implement bisection robust:
```
def project_continuous(h):
    h=np.asarray(h,float).copy()
    n=len(h); target=n/2.0
    h=np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
    s=float(h.sum())
    if abs(s-target) < 1e-12:
       return h
    lo=float(np.min(h))-1.0
    hi=float(np.max(h))+1.0
    # We want h=clip(x - lambda,0,1). If lambda increases, sum decreases.
    # At lo, probably sum>target? If lo very low, all 1 -> n > target. At hi, all 0 ->0.
    for _ in range(100):
       mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
       y=np.clip(h-mid,0.0,1.0)
       sy=float(y.sum())
       if sy > target:
          lo=mid
       else:
          hi=mid
    y=np.clip(h-mid,0.0,1.0)
    return y
```
If target integer and x near 0/1, there may be many same; bisection picks one. Good.

Now, `corr_shifts_float` maybe called many times in SLSQP. Direct Python loop over 2n-1 dots. For n=250, 499 dots each ~average 125 length -> 62k mult. 1000 calls ->62M, okay. But Python loop overhead 500k, okay. Could use FFT for speed but not needed.
`corr_shifts_int` similar but int. Direct loop.

Potential issue in SLSQP: It may call objective and gradient with x slightly outside [0,1]. We clip. But gradient clipped: if x is outside, subgradient of clipped x not wrt x; SLSQP may be confused near bounds. SLSQP with bounds should maintain. To be safer, we can project x into [0,1] in feasible sense? Not. Use clip.

Now, SLSQP maxiter. If min fail, returns x0. We project. Good.
`minimize` may emit warnings; no issue.

`make_initial_fallback(n)`:
```
def make_initial_fallback(n):
    n=max(2,int(n))
    h=np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*(2.0/float(n))
    mask = (x < 0.25) | ((x >= 0.75) & (x < 1.25)) | (x >= 1.75)
    h[mask]=1.0
    target=n//2
    s=int(h.sum())
    if s > target:
       idx=np.argsort(-h)
       cnt=s-target
       for ii in range(cnt):
          h[int(idx[ii])]=0.0
    elif s < target:
       idx=np.argsort(h)
       cnt=target-s
       for ii in range(cnt):
          h[int(idx[ii])]=1.0
    return h
```
If n odd target floor, integral not 1; for fallback even. Use if no parent, n=250 even.

`resample_h`: If n=1? not.
```
def resample_h(h,n):
    old_n=len(h); n=int(n)
    if old_n == n: return np.asarray(h,float).copy()
    old_n=max(1,old_n)
    x_old=(np.arange(old_n)+0.5)*(2.0/old_n)
    x_new=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*(2.0/n)
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, np.asarray(h,float))
```

`project_continuous`: As above. If n=0? no.
`fix_sum_exact`: use `np.clip` out.

`ensure_feasible`: as above.

`c5_of`: If h int8, 1.0-h. For n=1? no.
```
def c5_of(h):
    h=np.asarray(h,float)
    n=len(h)
    if n == 0: return 0.0
    c=np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, mode='full')*(2.0/float(n))
    return float(np.max(c))
```

`to_binary_array`: if n=0? no.
```
def to_binary_array(h):
    h=np.asarray(h,float); n=len(h)
    target=n//2
    rb=(h > 0.5).astype(np.int8)
    s=int(rb.sum())
    if s == target:
       return rb.astype(np.int8)
    order=np.argsort(-h, kind='stable')
    out=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int8)
    out[order[:target]]=1
    return out
```
If target=0 for n=1, okay.

`random_k_swaps`: ensure size.

`corr_shifts_int`: For n maybe 0? no.
```
def corr_shifts_int(h):
    hb=np.asarray(h, dtype=np.int8)
    n=len(hb)
    off=n-1; L=2*n-1
    res=np.empty(L, dtype=np.int32)
    oneh=1 - hb
    for s in range(-off, n):
       if s>=0:
          ln=n-s
          val=np.dot(hb[:ln], oneh[s:s+ln])
       else:
          t=-s; ln=n-t
          val=np.dot(hb[t:], oneh[:ln])
       res[off+s] = int(val)
    return res
```
Dot int8 returns int64? int(val) okay.

`corr_shifts_float` similarly with float, `oneh=1.0-h`.

Now, run phases final:
```
def run(seed=42,budget_s=1100,**kwargs):
    t0=time.time(); budget=float(budget_s)
    deadline=t0+budget-2.0
    eff=max(60.0, budget-15.0)
    # parent
    ...
    best_h=ensure_feasible(h_par); best_c=c5_of(best_h); best_n=len(best_h)
    # choose continuous n even
    n_con = best_n if best_n%2==0 else best_n+1
    h_con0 = prepare_resolution(best_h, n_con) if n_con != best_n else best_h.copy()
    p0=t0+0.35*eff
    if time.time() < p0-1:
       h1,c1=slsqp_phase(h_con0, p0, int(seed)+1)
       best_h,best_c,best_n=update_best(h1,best_h,best_c,best_n)
    p1=t0+0.42*eff
    # binary parent n_con if n_con <=600
    if n_con <= 800 and time.time() < p1-1:
       h1,c1=binary_search_wrap(h_con0, p1, int(seed)+11, steepest_burst=15.0, sa_burst=15.0)
       best_h,best_c,best_n=update_best(h1,best_h,best_c,best_n)
    # high res 2*n_con
    n_high1 = 2*n_con
    if n_high1 > 1200: n_high1 = n_con
    p2=t0+0.72*eff
    if n_high1 >= n_con and time.time() < p2-1:
       h1=prepare_resolution(best_h, n_high1)
       h1,c1=binary_search_wrap(h1, p2, int(seed)+17, steepest_burst=20.0, sa_burst=30.0)
       best_h,best_c,best_n=update_best(h1,best_h,best_c,best_n)
    # phase D: additional SLSQP or high2
    n_high2 = 4*n_con
    if n_high2 > 1200 or n_high2 == n_high1: n_high2 = n_high1
    if n_high2 != n_high1 and time.time() < deadline-5:
       # use short SA only maybe
       h1=prepare_resolution(best_h, n_high2)
       h1,c1=binary_search_wrap(h1, min(deadline, t0+0.90*eff), int(seed)+23, steepest_burst=0.0, sa_burst=40.0)
       update
    # final SLSQP on best n_con if time
    if time.time() < deadline-3:
       h1=prepare_resolution(best_h, n_con)
       h1,c1=slsqp_phase(h1, deadline-1, int(seed)+31)
       update
    # final ensure
    best_h=ensure_feasible(best_h)
    best_c=c5_of(best_h)
    return best_h, float(best_c), len(best_h)
```

```
def slsqp_run_once(x0, alpha, maxiter):
    global _ALPHA, _TARGET
    _ALPHA = float(alpha)
    _TARGET = float(len(x0) / 2.0)
    x0 = np.asarray(x0, float).copy()
    n = len(x0)
    bounds = []
    for i in range(n):
        bounds.append((0.0, 1.0))
    cons = [{'type': 'eq', 'fun': slsq_constraint}]
    try:
        res = minimize(slsq_objective, x0, jac=slsq_gradient, method='SLSQP',
                       bounds=bounds, constraints=cons,
                       options={'maxiter': int(maxiter), 'ftol': 1e-14})
        if hasattr(res, 'x'):
            return project_continuous(res['x'])
    except Exception:
        pass
    return project_continuous(x0)
```

```python
import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.optimize import minimize

_ALPHA = 1.0
_TARGET = 0.0


def make_initial_fallback(n):
    n = max(2, int(n))
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    x = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) * (2.0 / float(n))
    mask = (x < 0.25) | ((x >= 0.75) & (x < 1.25)) | (x >= 1.75)
    h[mask] = 1.0
    target = n // 2
    s = int(h.sum())
    if s > target:
        idx = np.argsort(-h)
        cnt = s - target
        for ii in range(cnt):
            h[int(idx[ii])] = 0.0
    elif s < target:
        idx = np.argsort(h)
        cnt = target - s
        for ii in range(cnt):
            h[int(idx[ii])] = 1.0
    return h


def resample_h(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    old_n = len(h)
    n = int(n)
    if old_n == n:
        return h.copy()
    old_n = max(1, old_n)
    x_old = (np.arange(old_n) + 0.5) * (2.0 / float(old_n))
    x_new = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) * (2.0 / float(n))
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, h)


def project_continuous(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    n = len(h)
    if n == 0:
        return h
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    s = float(h.sum())
    if abs(s - target) < 1e-12:
        return h
    lo = float(np.min(h)) - 1.0
    hi = float(np.max(h)) + 1.0
    mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
    for _ in range(100):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        y = np.clip(h - mid, 0.0, 1.0)
        sy = float(y.sum())
        if sy > target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    return np.clip(h - mid, 0.0, 1.0)


def fix_sum_exact(h):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float, copy=True)
    n = len(h)
    if n == 0:
        return h
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    for _ in range(60):
        diff = target - float(h.sum())
        if abs(diff) < 1e-14:
            break
        mask = (h > 1e-12) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-12)
        cnt = int(mask.sum())
        if cnt == 0:
            break
        h[mask] += diff / float(cnt)
        np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    return h


def ensure_feasible(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    n = len(h)
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    if n > 0 and abs(float(h.sum()) - target) > 1e-7:
        h = project_continuous(h)
    h = fix_sum_exact(h)
    return h


def c5_of(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = len(h)
    if n == 0:
        return 0.0
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full") * (2.0 / float(n))
    return float(np.max(c))


def to_binary_array(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = len(h)
    target = n // 2
    rb = (h > 0.5).astype(np.int8)
    s = int(rb.sum())
    if s == target:
        return rb.astype(np.int8)
    order = np.argsort(-h, kind="stable")
    out = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int8)
    if target > 0:
        out[order[:target]] = 1
    return out


def random_k_swaps(h, k, seed):
    hb = to_binary_array(h).astype(np.int8)
    zeros = np.flatnonzero(hb == 0).astype(np.int64).copy()
    ones = np.flatnonzero(hb == 1).astype(np.int64).copy()
    if zeros.size == 0 or ones.size == 0:
        return hb.astype(float)
    rng = np.random.RandomState(int(seed))
    for _ in range(int(k)):
        if zeros.size == 0 or ones.size == 0:
            break
        a = int(rng.randint(zeros.size))
        b = int(rng.randint(ones.size))
        i = int(zeros[a])
        j = int(ones[b])
        hb[i] = 1
        hb[j] = 0
        zeros[a] = j
        ones[b] = i
    return hb.astype(float)


def prepare_resolution(h, n_target):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n_old = len(h)
    n_target = int(n_target)
    if n_target <= 0:
        return h.copy()
    if n_old == n_target:
        return h.copy()
    if n_target % n_old == 0:
        b = (h > 0.5).astype(float)
        if np.allclose(h, b, atol=1e-8, rtol=0.0):
            return np.repeat(b, n_target // n_old)
    return resample_h(h, n_target)


def update_best(hcand, best_h, best_c, best_n):
    cand = np.asarray(hcand, dtype=float)
    if len(cand) == 0:
        return best_h, best_c, best_n
    cand = ensure_feasible(cand)
    c = c5_of(cand)
    if c < best_c - 1e-14:
        return cand, float(c), len(cand)
    return best_h, best_c, best_n


def corr_shifts_int(h):
    hb = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.int8)
    n = len(hb)
    off = n - 1
    L = 2 * n - 1
    res = np.empty(L, dtype=np.int32)
    oneh = 1 - hb
    for s in range(-off, n):
        if s >= 0:
            ln = n - s
            val = np.dot(hb[:ln], oneh[s:s + ln])
        else:
            t = -s
            ln = n - t
            val = np.dot(hb[t:], oneh[:ln])
        res[off + s] = int(val)
    return res


def corr_shifts_float(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = len(h)
    off = n - 1
    L = 2 * n - 1
    res = np.empty(L, dtype=float)
    oneh = 1.0 - h
    for s in range(-off, n):
        if s >= 0:
            ln = n - s
            res[off + s] = np.dot(h[:ln], oneh[s:s + ln])
        else:
            t = -s
            ln = n - t
            res[off + s] = np.dot(h[t:], oneh[:ln])
    return res


def swap_delta(h, i, j):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.int8)
    n = len(h)
    off = n - 1
    L = 2 * n - 1
    delta = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.int32)
    oneh = 1 - h
    hrev = h[::-1]

    delta[off - i:off - i + n] += oneh
    delta[i:n + i] -= hrev

    delta[off - j:off - j + n] -= oneh
    delta[j:n + j] += hrev

    delta[off] -= 2
    delta[off + j - i] += 1
    delta[off + i - j] += 1
    return delta


def find_best_swap(h, corr):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.int8)
    corr = np.asarray(corr, dtype=np.int32)
    n = len(h)
    off = n - 1
    L = 2 * n - 1
    zeros = np.flatnonzero(h == 0)
    ones = np.flatnonzero(h == 1)
    mz = int(zeros.size)
    mo = int(ones.size)
    if mz == 0 or mo == 0:
        return None

    oneh = 1 - h
    hrev = h[::-1]

    V = np.empty((mo, L), dtype=np.int32)
    for bi in range(mo):
        j = int(ones[bi])
        row = V[bi]
        row[:] = 0
        row[off - j:off - j + n] -= oneh
        row[j:n + j] += hrev

    cur = int(corr.max())
    D = np.empty((mo, L), dtype=np.int32)
    U = np.empty(L, dtype=np.int32)
    add = np.empty(L, dtype=np.int32)
    bas = np.empty(mo, dtype=np.int32)
    pt = np.empty(mo, dtype=np.int32)
    p0 = np.empty(mo, dtype=np.int32)
    SENT = -1000000000

    best_val = cur
    best_i = -1
    best_j = -1

    for ai in range(mz):
        i = int(zeros[ai])
        U[:] = 0
        U[off - i:off - i + n] += oneh
        U[i:n + i] -= hrev

        np.add(corr, U, out=add)
        pos_lags = off + (ones - i)
        neg_lags = off + (i - ones)

        np.add(V, add, out=D)
        p0[:] = V[:, off]
        p0 += add[off] - 2

        D[:, off] = SENT
        np.max(D, axis=1, out=bas)

        np.add(V[:, pos_lags], add[pos_lags], out=pt)
        pt += 1
        np.maximum(bas, pt, out=bas)

        np.add(V[:, neg_lags], add[neg_lags], out=pt)
        pt += 1
        np.maximum(bas, pt, out=bas)

        np.maximum(bas, p0, out=bas)

        idx = int(np.argmin(bas))
        val = int(bas[idx])
        if val < best_val:
            best_val = val
            best_i = i
            best_j = int(ones[idx])

    if best_val < cur - 1e-6:
        return (best_i, best_j, best_val)
    return None


def steepest_repeated(h, deadline, max_moves=1000000):
    n = len(h)
    if n <= 1:
        return np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 0
    hb = to_binary_array(h).astype(np.int8)
    corr = corr_shifts_int(hb)
    cur = int(corr.max())
    recompute_every = 32 if n <= 300 else 64
    moves = 0

    while time.time() + 0.05 < float(deadline):
        if moves >= max_moves:
            break
        res = find_best_swap(hb, corr)
        if res is None:
            break
        i, j, val = res
        if val >= cur - 1e-6:
            break
        delta = swap_delta(hb, i, j)
        hb[i] = 1
        hb[j] = 0
        corr += delta
        cur = val
        moves += 1
        if moves % recompute_every == 0:
            corr = corr_shifts_int(hb)
            cur = int(corr.max())

    return hb.astype(float), cur


def simulate_binary(h, deadline, seed, tc0=0.005):
    n = len(h)
    if n <= 1:
        return np.asarray(h, dtype=float), c5_of(h)
    hb = to_binary_array(h).astype(np.int8)
    off = n - 1
    L = 2 * n - 1
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)

    corr = corr_shifts_int(hb)
    cur = int(corr.max())
    cur_c = cur * dx
    best_h = hb.copy()
    best_c = cur_c

    zeros = np.flatnonzero(hb == 0).astype(np.int64).copy()
    ones = np.flatnonzero(hb == 1).astype(np.int64).copy()
    if zeros.size == 0 or ones.size == 0:
        return best_h.astype(float), best_c

    oneh = 1 - hb
    hrev = hb[::-1]
    delta = np.empty(L, dtype=np.int32)
    tmp = np.empty(L, dtype=np.int32)
    rng = np.random.RandomState(int(seed))

    t_start = time.time()
    total = max(float(deadline) - t_start, 1e-9)

    while time.time() + 0.01 < float(deadline):
        frac = (time.time() - t_start) / total
        if frac >= 1.0:
            break
        T = float(tc0) * ((1.0 - frac) ** 2)

        a = int(rng.randint(zeros.size))
        b = int(rng.randint(ones.size))
        i = int(zeros[a])
        j = int(ones[b])

        delta[:] = 0
        delta[off - i:off - i + n] += oneh
        delta[i:n + i] -= hrev
        delta[off - j:off - j + n] -= oneh
        delta[j:n + j] += hrev
        delta[off] -= 2
        delta[off + j - i] += 1
        delta[off + i - j] += 1

        np.add(corr, delta, out=tmp)
        newmax = int(tmp.max())
        new_c = newmax * dx

        accept = False
        if new_c < cur_c:
            accept = True
        elif T > 0.0 and new_c < cur_c + T:
            accept = True

        if accept:
            hb[i] = 1
            hb[j] = 0
            oneh[i] = 0
            oneh[j] = 1
            zeros[a] = j
            ones[b] = i
            corr += delta
            cur = newmax
            cur_c = new_c
            if cur_c < best_c:
                best_c = cur_c
                best_h = hb.copy()

    return best_h.astype(float), best_c


def binary_search_wrap(h_init, deadline, seed, steepest_burst=25.0, sa_burst=40.0):
    n = len(h_init)
    if n <= 1:
        return np.asarray(h_init, dtype=float), c5_of(h_init)
    if n % 2 != 0:
        n += 1
        h_init = resample_h(h_init, n)

    h0 = to_binary_array(h_init).astype(float)
    best_h = h0.copy()
    best_c = c5_of(best_h)
    can_steepest = (n <= 600)
    attempt = 0

    while time.time() + 3.0 < float(deadline):
        attempt += 1

        if can_steepest and steepest_burst > 0.5 and time.time() + 5.0 < float(deadline):
            remain = float(deadline) - time.time()
            burst = min(float(steepest_burst), remain - 4.0)
            if burst > 0.5:
                end = time.time() + burst
                h_out, cnt = steepest_repeated(best_h, end)
                cval = float(cnt) * (2.0 / float(n))
                if cval < best_c - 1e-12:
                    best_c = cval
                    best_h = h_out.copy()

        if time.time() + 4.0 < float(deadline):
            remain = float(deadline) - time.time()
            burst = min(float(sa_burst), remain - 3.0)
            if burst > 0.5:
                end = time.time() + burst
                h_out, csa = simulate_binary(best_h, end, int(seed) + attempt)
                if csa < best_c - 1e-12:
                    best_c = csa
                    best_h = h_out.copy()

        if time.time() + 4.0 < float(deadline):
            remain = float(deadline) - time.time()
            burst = min(0.7 * float(sa_burst), remain - 3.0)
            if burst > 0.5:
                k = 2 + (attempt % 5)
                h_pert = random_k_swaps(best_h, k, int(seed) + 1000 + attempt)
                end = time.time() + burst
                h_out, csa = simulate_binary(h_pert, end, int(seed) + 2000 + attempt)
                if csa < best_c - 1e-12:
                    best_c = csa
                    best_h = h_out.copy()

        if can_steepest and steepest_burst > 0.5 and time.time() + 5.0 < float(deadline):
            remain = float(deadline) - time.time()
            burst = min(0.5 * float(steepest_burst), remain - 4.0)
            if burst > 0.5:
                end = time.time() + burst
                h_out, cnt = steepest_repeated(best_h, end)
                cval = float(cnt) * (2.0 / float(n))
                if cval < best_c - 1e-12:
                    best_c = cval
                    best_h = h_out.copy()

    best_h = ensure_feasible(best_h)
    best_c = c5_of(best_h)
    return best_h, best_c


def weighted_gradient(h, w):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=float)
    n = len(h)
    grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    off = n - 1
    for s in range(-off, n):
        ws = float(w[off + s])
        if ws == 0.0:
            continue
        if s >= 0:
            ln = n - s
            if ln > 0:
                grad[:ln] += ws * (1.0 - h[s:s + ln])
                grad[s:] -= ws * h[:ln]
        else:
            t = -s
            ln = n - t
            if ln > 0:
                grad[t:] += ws * (1.0 - h[:ln])
                grad[:ln] -= ws * h[t:]
    return grad


def soft_c_and_grad(h, alpha):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = len(h)
    c = corr_shifts_float(h)
    M = float(np.max(c))
    alpha = max(float(alpha), 1.0)
    temp = 1.0 / alpha
    z = (c - M) / temp
    z = np.clip(z, -745.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        active = (c >= M - 1e-12)
        if int(active.sum()) == 0:
            active[np.argmax(c)] = True
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[active] = 1.0 / float(active.sum())
        soft = M
    else:
        w = e / s
        soft = M + temp * np.log(s)
    g = weighted_gradient(h, w)
    return float(soft), g


def slsq_objective(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    if x.size == 0:
        return 0.0
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        return 1e100
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1.0)
    v, _ = soft_c_and_grad(x, _ALPHA)
    return float(v)


def slsq_gradient(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    if x.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=float)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        return np.zeros_like(x, dtype=float)
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1.0)
    _, g = soft_c_and_grad(x, _ALPHA)
    return g


def slsq_constraint(x):
    return float(np.sum(x)) - float(_TARGET)


def slsqp_run_once(x0, alpha, maxiter):
    global _ALPHA, _TARGET
    x0 = np.asarray(x0, dtype=float).copy()
    n = len(x0)
    if n <= 1:
        return project_continuous(x0)
    _ALPHA = float(max(float(alpha), 1.0))
    _TARGET = 0.5 * float(n)
    bounds = []
    for _ in range(n):
        bounds.append((0.0, 1.0))
    constraints = [{'type': 'eq', 'fun': slsq_constraint}]
    try:
        res = minimize(
            slsq_objective,
            x0,
            jac=slsq_gradient,
            method='SLSQP',
            bounds=bounds,
            constraints=constraints,
            options={'maxiter': max(10, int(maxiter)), 'ftol': 1e-14}
        )
        if hasattr(res, 'x') and res.x is not None:
            return project_continuous(res.x)
    except Exception:
        pass
    return project_continuous(x0)


def slsqp_phase(h_init, deadline, seed):
    h = ensure_feasible(h_init)
    n = len(h)
    if n > 350:
        c = c5_of(h)
        return h, c

    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = c5_of(best_h)

    chain = [
        (20.0, 80),
        (50.0, 80),
        (100.0, 100),
        (200.0, 100),
        (500.0, 80),
        (1000.0, 70),
    ]

    for alpha, maxit in chain:
        if time.time() + 2.0 > float(deadline):
            break
        h1 = slsqp_run_once(h, alpha, maxit)
        c1 = c5_of(h1)
        if c1 < best_c - 1e-14:
            best_c = c1
            best_h = h1.copy()
            h = h1.copy()
        else:
            h = best_h.copy()

    rng = np.random.RandomState(int(seed))
    alpha_cycle = [100.0, 300.0, 1000.0, 50.0, 500.0, 1000.0]
    count = 0

    while time.time() + 2.0 < float(deadline):
        count += 1
        amp = 0.01 + 0.03 * rng.rand()
        if count % 6 == 0:
            amp = 0.08
        x0 = project_continuous(best_h + amp * rng.randn(n))
        alpha = alpha_cycle[count % len(alpha_cycle)]
        h1 = slsqp_run_once(x0, alpha, 80)
        c1 = c5_of(h1)
        if c1 < best_c - 1e-14:
            best_c = c1
            best_h = h1.copy()

    best_h = ensure_feasible(best_h)
    best_c = c5_of(best_h)
    return best_h, best_c


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    budget = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = t0 + budget - 2.0
    total = max(1.0, deadline - t0)

    try:
        h_par = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float)
        if h_par.size == 0:
            raise ValueError
    except Exception:
        h_par = None

    if h_par is None:
        n_par = 250
        h_par = make_initial_fallback(n_par)

    best_n = len(h_par)
    best_h = ensure_feasible(h_par)
    best_c = c5_of(best_h)

    if best_n % 2 != 0:
        n_con = best_n + 1
    else:
        n_con = best_n
    h_con0 = prepare_resolution(best_h, n_con) if n_con != best_n else best_h.copy()

    seed = int(seed)

    pA = min(deadline, t0 + 0.40 * total)
    if time.time() + 2.0 < pA:
        h1, c1 = slsqp_phase(h_con0, pA, seed + 1)
        best_h, best_c, best_n = update_best(h1, best_h, best_c, best_n)

    pB = min(deadline, t0 + 0.48 * total)
    if n_con <= 800 and time.time() + 2.0 < pB:
        h1 = prepare_resolution(best_h, n_con)
        h1, c1 = binary_search_wrap(h1, pB, seed + 11, steepest_burst=15.0, sa_burst=15.0)
        best_h, best_c, best_n = update_best(h1, best_h, best_c, best_n)

    pC = min(deadline, t0 + 0.76 * total)
    n_high1 = 2 * n_con
    if n_high1 > 1200:
        n_high1 = n_con
    if n_high1 >= n_con and time.time() + 2.0 < pC:
        h1 = prepare_resolution(best_h, n_high1)
        h1, c1 = binary_search_wrap(h1, pC, seed + 17, steepest_burst=22.0, sa_burst=32.0)
        best_h, best_c, best_n = update_best(h1, best_h, best_c, best_n)

    pD1 = min(deadline, t0 + 0.91 * total)
    n_high2 = 4 * n_con
    if n_high2 > 1200 or n_high2 == n_high1:
        n_high2 = n_high1
    if n_high2 > n_high1 and time.time() + 4.0 < pD1:
        h1 = prepare_resolution(best_h, n_high2)
        h1, c1 = binary_search_wrap(h1, pD1, seed + 23, steepest_burst=0.0, sa_burst=45.0)
        best_h, best_c, best_n = update_best(h1, best_h, best_c, best_n)

    if time.time() + 2.0 < deadline:
        h1 = prepare_resolution(best_h, n_con)
        h1, c1 = slsqp_phase(h1, deadline, seed + 31)
        best_h, best_c, best_n = update_best(h1, best_h, best_c, best_n)

    best_h = ensure_feasible(best_h)
    best_c = c5_of(best_h)
    return best_h, float(best_c), len(best_h)
```
